ASEAN経済は国ごとにばらつき。ベトナムは輸出や
鉱工業生産の好調を背景に高成⾧持続。フィリピン
では食料品やエネルギー価格高騰が消費の重石に。
タイでも原油高を背景にインフレ圧力が強まる
APAC
賃金改善が続く一方、個人消費の回復に弱さ。実
質消費支出は前年比減少が継続。消費者マインド

も小幅悪化し、物価高への警戒が根強い。景気動
向指数は低下したものの、基調判断は改善を維持
日
本
先週（10/5～10/12）発表の主な経済指標
米国では金融政策運営の変化と財政懸念、欧州では財政不
安が金利上昇圧力に。欧州では⾧期金利上昇による景気下
押し圧力が、ECBの追加利上げの必要性を低下させる可能性
金利・コスト上昇などの逆風下でも、設備投資意欲は旺盛。家
計の節約志向や企業間の格差は残るものの、価格転嫁・賃上
げも継続しており、日銀の想定するシナリオに沿った動き

米国：中間選挙を目前に住宅市場は軟調、アフォーダビリティへの不満が共和党への逆風に
 住宅ローン金利は足元で再び上昇
― 借入コストの負担増により、足元の住宅ローン申請件数は減少
 物価上昇が生活コストを押し上げるなか、金利上昇による住宅取得環境の悪化も家計の不満を助長する要因に
― トランプ大統領の生活費への政策対応は約7割が「期待より悪い」と評価しており、支持率は低下基調
―11月に控える中間選挙では、アフォーダビリティへの対応が引き続き主要な論点

米国：金融政策・財政の両面から米長期金利に上昇圧力
 住宅ローン金利の基準となる米国10年債利回りは9月以降水準を切り上げ、足元（10/6時点）では5.3％近傍に
― 構成要素をみると、追加利上げ観測の高まりを受け、実質予想短期金利が上昇。ウォーシュFRB議長の就任以降、フォ
ワードガイダンスの後退を背景に、経済指標への金利の感応度が高まっている可能性
― 加えて、足元ではタームプレミアムも拡大。中間選挙を控えた家計支援策への思惑などから、財政懸念が意識されてい

る模様。トランプ大統領は10/2、Medicare加入者2,000万人超への90ドルの一時金支給を発表
5

欧州：長期金利上昇を受け、一部ECB高官は追加利上げの必要性低下を指摘
6
 フランスでは国内政治・財政面の不安から長期金利が上昇。イタリア・ギリシャなど周辺の高債務国にも波及
― フランス国債の対独利回り差は急拡大し、一時欧州債務危機（2012年）以来の水準に。その後、大統領選の有力候補と
目されるルペン氏（国民連合）が財政赤字を縮小する意欲を示し、拡大はひとまず一服
―ECBによる介入（国債買い入れ等）の可能性については、複数の高官が検討段階にないとの見方を示す
 長期金利の上昇は需要を抑制。ECB高官からは追加利上げの必要性が低下するとの見方も
― エネルギー高によるインフレ圧力と金利上昇による景気下押し圧力が併存。ECBの政策判断は一段と難しくなる可能性
仏長期金利上昇の経済影響（左）と直近のECB高官発言（右）
（出所）LSEGより、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

### Page 8

7
日本：金利・コスト上昇下でも企業活動は底堅く、設備投資意欲の強さを再確認
 10月のさくらレポートではAI関連需要の拡大とその裾野の広がりを背景に、企業活動の底堅さを確認
― 同一の企業コメントに登場するテーマの組み合わせを集計した共起分析では、「金利×設備投資」の組み合わせが増加。
金利上昇への意識が強まる一方、成長・省人化投資の旺盛なニーズは継続
 賃上げや資産効果が消費を下支えする一方、物価高を背景とした消費の二極化が継続
― 企業は価格転嫁を積極化するも、消費者の節約志向が根強く、商品競争力や顧客層によって転嫁余力に格差
⇒景気・物価は強弱入り混じるも、日銀の想定するシナリオに概ね沿った展開。12月利上げ見通しは不変
企業コメントの共起関係の変化
（注） 同一の箇条書きコメントに、双方のテーマのキーワードがある場合を1件として計上。
なお、テーマ間の因果関係を意味するものではない
（出所） 日本銀行「地域経済報告」より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成
企業コメント概要
0 10 20 30

金利 × 設備投資
価格転嫁×企業収益
AI関連 × 設備投資
中東情勢 × 価格転嫁
節約志向 × 消費・販売
7月
10月
（件）
設備投資における金利負
担、企業収益における価
格転嫁の重要性を示唆
 AI関連需要は想定を上回るペースで拡大。半導
体製造装置に加え、工作機械や光ファイバー関連
製品など幅広い分野に波及
 金利・建設コストの上昇下でも、競争力維持に必
要な投資を継続。一方、採算悪化や調達コスト増
から投資を縮小・延期する動きも
設備投資・生産
 原材料・人件費の上昇を受け、企業の価格改定は
機動化。値上げ頻度を引き上げる企業がみられる
一方、消費者の価格感応度も高まる
 価格転嫁が進み賃上げ原資を確保できる企業が
ある一方、転嫁不足から利益を削って賃上げを続
ける企業も
価格転嫁・賃金
 株高による資産効果や賃上げを背景に、高額品・
高機能家電・旅行などの需要は堅調
 一方、物価高の長期化を受け、高年層を中心に生
活防衛意識が強まる。低価格品へのシフトや値上

世界経済：エネルギー・食料の供給ショックによる負担は、所得階層や国によって格差
 生活費高騰に伴うインフレの影響は所得階層により格差
― 食料・エネルギー支出の割合が高い低所得層は負担が大。IMFは低所得層に絞った所得移転が望ましい対応と指摘
 エネルギー・食料の供給ショックによる影響は国家間でも格差
― 先進国では、エネルギー供給ショックによる消費の下押し効果は低所得層で特に大きい
― 食料品への支出割合が高い新興国・低所得途上国では、食料供給ショックによる消費の下押し効果が相対的に大きい

 タイでは過去にも大規模洪水が発生。2011年には、日系自動車企業の部材調達が滞り、日本国内生産に影響した経験も
―2011年の洪水発生時には、工場の被災や物流停滞、人員不足が発生。タイの製造業稼働率は一時30％近く低下
― 当時浸水した工業団地では日系企業約450社が被災。タイ製部品の調達難により、日本でも完成車が一時減産に

―10月頭時点では各社、大規模な設備被害や減産は見込まず、日本の部材調達への影響も限定的となる見込み
― ただし、10月中旬にかけ更なる大雨が予想されており、操業停止の長期化や被害地域の拡大には依然警戒が必要

日本：個人消費は耐久財特需が剥落。今後は良好な所得環境を背景に緩やかに拡大
12
 8月の消費活動指数は、耐久財特需の剥落などにより、前年比＋0.9％と伸びが鈍化
― 耐久財消費は春先の自動車特需の反動から減少。食品などの非耐久財も、根強い節約志向を背景に弱含みが継続
― 底堅く推移してきたサービス消費も、台風・大雨や猛暑による外出控えの影響などから拡大ペースが鈍化
 今後は良好な所得環境が消費を下支え。個人消費は緩慢なペースながら、底堅く推移する見通し
― 実質所定内給与はプラス圏の伸びを維持しており、基調的な所得環境は良好。増加基調の購買力が消費を下支え
― ただし、インフレ率の再上昇で消費の回復ペースは緩慢。マインドの改善が一服したことも回復ペースを抑える要因に

よる海外消費（アウトバウンド消費）を加えるための調整要因。アウトバウンド消費は
SNAの家計最終消費支出に含まれるが、GDPの計算上は輸入として控除される

日本：景気ウォッチャーは消費の緩やかな持ち直しを示唆も、家計・企業とも先行きには慎重
13
 家計動向（現状判断DI）は改善。9月の消費回復を示唆するも、回復ペースは緩慢
― 特にサービス関連の上昇が顕著。8月に増勢が一服したサービス消費は、大型連休や秋の行楽需要を受け持ち直し
― ただし、先行き判断DIは低下しており、個人消費全体でみれば緩やかな回復であることを示唆
 一方、企業動向は、現状・先行きとも慎重。景気回復の勢いは強くない
― 企業動向（現状判断DI）は製造業・非製造業ともに低下。AI関連など需要は堅調も、資材高などの供給制約が重石に

― 先行き判断についても、原材料高や金利上昇、中東情勢の不透明感などが重石となっている模様

か？

## Document 026: 20260930_投資週報_經濟動能穩健，延續科技輪動行情

Source PDF: all report pdf/20260930_投資週報_經濟動能穩健，延續科技輪動行情.pdf

### Page 1

金融市場投資週報                 2026 年 9 月 30 日
上週重點訊息
◼ 美國企業投資動能維持韌性 ：8 月標普全球美國綜合 PMI 升至 58.4，創2021 年 7 月來高點 ，
製造業、服務業同步擴張至 57 和 58.7； 8 月扣除飛機與國防的核心資本財訂單月增 1.6%，
高於前月修正後的 0.6%。其中，初級金屬、機械及電機設備訂單均呈現成長，顯示企業資本
支出、AI 投資與國防需求仍具韌性，持續支撐美國經濟成長。
◼ 美中同意貿易休兵再延長兩個月：美中同意調降雙邊總值 600 億美元商品的關稅，涵蓋美國

玉米、化妝品及中國家電、玩具等產品，並延長貿易休兵至明年 1 月 10 日，同時中國承諾繼
續供應稀土並擴大採購美國農產品與煤礦，美國則繼續暫停實施 50%穿透性規則；AI 方面，
美中雙方雖未就 AI 監管議題達成共識 ， 但同意建立風險溝通機制 ， 並於11 月底前展開對話 。
◼ Meta 旗下 AI 代理人 Muse 登頂排行榜：Meta 新款個人 AI 代理 Muse 上線後迅速累積逾
250 萬次下載，並登上美國 App Store 下載排行榜首位。相較過去生成式 AI 以問答與內容生
成為主，Muse 可進一步協助安排日程、搜尋資訊、處理文件及完成交易，代表 AI 正從提升
效率工具逐步走向數位勞動力，市場評價焦點也將逐漸由 AI 基礎設施擴散至應用軟體業者。
市場展望
◼ AI 需求持續擴張，科技供應鏈成長動能延續：AI 基礎建設需求持續擴張，帶動 HBM、伺服器
DRAM、 先進製程與封裝等關鍵供應鏈維持成長動能 ， 市場表現由全面上漲逐步轉向結構性分
化。若 AI 伺服器需求持續成長且 CSP 資本支出維持高檔，具供給優勢、定價能力及較高訂單

能見度之企業，其營運表現有機會獲得支撐。整體而言，科技產業獲利表現仍具關注度，惟後
續仍須留意全球景氣、產業供需變化、政策監管及市場評價調整等風險因素。
◼ 債券佈局可關注中天期美國公債及投等債：中天期美國公債及投資級債仍值得持續關注：**雖
然貨幣政策風險下降有助於穩定長期通膨預期 ， 但政府與企業發債供給增加所帶來的排擠效應 ，
以及美國經濟成長展現韌性，仍可能使長天期利率面臨上行壓力。因此，在當前利率環境下，
中天期債券於票息收益與存續期間風險之間相對較為均衡。投資級債方面，彭博美國投資級公
司債指數利差約 80 個基點，雖然利差偏窄，但高基準利率環境仍提供相對較高的收益水準。
因此，在目前市場環境下，中天期美國公債及投資級債仍為值得持續觀察的債券資產類別，惟
後續表現仍須留意利率走勢、信用利差變化及經濟成長動能等因素。

市場焦點
美國
科技股
• 美國企業獲利維持強勁 ，背後主要受經濟展望穩健及 AI資本支出熱潮支撐 。從獲利來看 ，市
場共識預期標普500企業EPS成長率2026年達28%，2027年及2028年則分別為20%及16%，
雖呈現逐步降溫 ，但仍維持雙位數增長 。標普500預期本益比已由約 23倍降至19倍，且並非
主要來自股價修正 ，而是企業獲利預期上調所致 ，在企業獲利維持成長 、AI投資持續挹注及
估值已較高點收斂的情況下，標普500仍具中長期配置價值。
美債
外匯對沖視角
• 在全球公債遭遇拋售潮與通膨陰影下 ，美債因利率期間結構與匯率優勢 ，今年以美元計價總
回報優於多數G7主權債，儘管海外私人機構投資人正在等待長端利率落底訊號以重返市場 ，
對美元資金或以美元計價為主的投資人而言 ，當前美債名目殖利率仍處於數十年高位 ，具備

相當良好的長期鎖利價值，但價格波動風險考量下，建議債券佈局仍以中短天期為主。
台股
• 8月景氣對策燈號續亮紅燈，綜合判斷分數41分，較前月持平，對應的燈號仍是代表景氣熱絡
的紅燈，連續第9個月亮出紅燈，追平史上最長「連紅」紀錄，顯示國內經濟維持成長。
• 根據歷史統計第四季台股上漲的機率接近八成 ，除了AI投資趨勢未減 ，年底集團作帳行情以
及美國11月期中選後不確定性下降有望支撐台股多頭情緒 。群益投顧樂觀看待台股第四季表
現，有望上看53,000點。此外，彭博綜合統計台灣加權指數12個月目標價達59,688點。

就業增幅明顯放緩，降低Fed短期再升息急迫性
• 非農明顯降溫，勞動市場尚未出現全面惡化：9月非農僅增2.9萬人，遠低於預期，且7、8月合計下修6萬人；失業率升
至4.2%，但勞參率同步升至 61.8%，家戶調查就業增加 40.6萬人，顯示失業率上升部分來自勞動供給增加 ，其中營建、
製造業仍分別增加1.1萬及0.9萬人，平均工時維持34.4小時，數據呈現招聘動能明顯放慢，但尚未出現全面性就業惡化。
• 薪資增速放緩，Fed短期政策壓力下降：9月平均時薪月增僅0.1%、年增3.0%，均低於前值；同時8月PCE年增3.4%、
核心PCE年增3.0%，顯示薪資通膨壓力有所緩和 ，但整體物價仍高於Fed 2%目標，在9月Fed升息1碼後，非農報告降
低Fed在10月連續升息的必要性，市場焦點轉向年底通膨與AI投資驅動的產業動能。

• 投資建議：短期關注美國中短天期投資級債券 ，就業降溫與薪資放緩有助降低短端利率上行壓力 ，但通膨仍高於目標 ，
因此不宜過度 預期長天期利率快速回落；股市則可續關注 AI基礎建設與資本支出相關產業 ，因營建及製造業就業仍具
韌性，顯示企業投資動能尚未明顯轉弱。
資料來源：U.S. Bureau of Labor Statistics， 2026.10.3

消費動能回升、通膨低於預期，內需韌性與物價壓力並存
• PCE低於預期、通膨降溫仍待確認：美國8月PCE物價指數月增0.3%、年增3.4%，核心PCE月增0.2%、年增3.0%，均低於
市場預期；其中核心PCE較修訂後7月的3.3%下降，顯示物價壓力有所緩解，但仍高於Fed 2%目標，此次年度更新回溯修
訂2021年以來資料，亦使歷史基期重新調整，後續仍須觀察通膨下降是否能延續。
• 消費回升、所得增幅偏緩：8月個人所得月增0.2%，個人消費支出（PCE）月增0.9%、實質消費成長0.6%，顯示內需仍具
韌性；惟消費增幅明顯高於所得 ，個人儲蓄率由7月4.6%降至4.1%，為2022年11月以來低點，反映家庭可支應消費的財
務緩衝持續縮小，後續消費動能仍須觀察。
• 後市觀察：PCE低於預期後，市場對10月再升息的預期明顯降溫，但長端美債殖利率仍受通膨、財政供給與期限溢價牽動

而維持高檔；建議可聚焦獲利成長與現金流穩健的優質大型成長股與短天期美債以掌握收益 ，同時留意儲蓄率下滑與 9月
消費者信心低迷可能抑制後續消費動能，動態調整部位並嚴控風險。
資料來源：U.S. Bureau of Economic Analysis， 2026.10.1

能源與AI推升長端利率，融資成本壓力延續
• 長端殖利率上行 、期限溢價成重要推力 ：近期
美債呈現短端與長端走勢分化 ，雖市場對 10月
Fed再升息預期因 8月PCE低於預期而明顯降溫 ，
10年期及30年期的美債殖利率仍偏高位 ，反映
通膨風險 、財政融資需求與期限溢價上升 ，長
天期無風險利率維持高檔 ，並推升房貸及企業
融資成本。
• 能源與AI投資推升通膨及資金需求 ：油價維持
高檔增加通膨黏著風險 ，AI基礎建設投資則帶
動企業資本支出與融資需求 ，兩者共同提高長
期資金需求；在財政赤字與美債供給仍大的環
境下，長端殖利率可能維持較高水準 ，進而提
高股票估值折現率及高槓桿企業的融資壓力。
• 後市觀察： 短期持續觀察油價 、10年及30年期
美債殖利率與期限溢價變化；債券配置仍建議

降低長天期債券部位以降低利率風險 ，優先布
局短天期高品質債券獲取穩健息收 ，股票則聚
焦具穩健現金流 、低財務槓桿與定價能力的企
業，以降低高利率環境下的估值與融資風險。
資料來源：Bloomberg，Federal Reserve Bank of New Tork ， 2026.10.1

AI需求大增，美光財測超過市場預期
• AI記憶體需求強勁，美光財測顯示景氣延續：美光科技9/30盤後公布財報，2026財年的第四季營收達542.3億美元，年增

379%，調整後EPS 33.42美元，均優於市場預期；公司並預估2027會計年度第一季營收615億美元、調整後EPS 38.15美元，
分別高於市場預期的567.7億美元及36.02美元。強勁財測反映AI資料中心持續推升HBM、伺服器DRAM及高速SSD需求。
隨Amazon、Alphabet等大型科技企業持續擴大AI基礎建設投資，記憶體需求仍具成長動能。
• 長約與產品升級，支撐中長期業績：美光目前已簽署36份多年期供應協議，涵蓋約三分之一營收並延伸至2030年，有助降
低記憶體產業週期波動；同時持續布局HBM4、HBM4E及高容量DDR5等產品。公司2026會計年度自由現金流達590億美元，
並預期2027、2028會計年度供需將進一步緊俏。整體而言，AI需求已由單一HBM擴展至伺服器記憶體與儲存產品，美光財
測亦顯示景氣延續性仍強，後續市場焦點將轉向擴產速度、資本支出及AI需求成長能否維持。
資料來源：美光，彭博，鉅亨網，2026.10.1

• AI驅動獲利成長，企業基本面仍具支撐：美國企業獲利維持強勁，背後主要受經濟展望穩健及AI資本支出熱潮支撐。從獲利
來看，市場共識預期標普500企業EPS成長率2026年達28%，2027年及2028年則分別為20%及16%，雖呈現逐步降溫，但
仍維持雙位數增長。其中，AI基礎建設與大型雲端業者仍是重要驅動來源，2026年對EPS成長的貢獻合計約19%，2027、
2028年仍分別貢獻14%及11%。
• 獲利上修降低估值壓力：標普500預期本益比已由約23倍降至19倍，且並非主要來自股價修正，而是企業獲利預期上調所致。
雖然未來EPS成長率若由高檔回落，市場仍可能面臨評價修正壓力，但在企業獲利維持成長、AI投資持續挹注及估值已較高
點收斂的情況下，標普500基本面仍具支撐。因此，短期應留意獲利增速放緩與市場持倉偏高所帶來的波動，但從獲利成長

與估值兩大面向觀察，標普500仍具中長期配置價值。

生產回升與價格上漲同步推動中國PMI改善
• 生產回升與價格上漲同步推動 中國PMI改善： 9月製造業PMI升至50.1%，較前月回升0.3個百分點，重返榮枯線以上，顯示
製造業景氣有所改善 ，主要受生產活動回暖及價格上漲帶動 ，中小企業景氣同步改善 ，但仍低於榮枯線，大型企業表現相對
較佳，高技術製造、裝備製造維持擴張 ，消費品PMI升至50.7%，重回景氣區間 ，另一方面，原材料及能源價格上漲推升企
業成本，上下游獲利差距持續擴大 。 非製造業PMI則由49.0%升至50.2%，服務業受節慶效應帶動回溫 ，建築業則受財政資

金投入，及基建建設推進而改善，不過需求復甦仍偏溫和，企業成本壓力尚未明顯減輕。
• 結論：第四季隨著基建投資、財政支出及房市支持政策逐步落地，經濟可望獲得支撐，但10月受長假因素影響，PMI可能短
期承壓。

台灣景氣燈號連9紅 台股Q4有望突破5萬點大關
• 8月景氣對策燈號續亮紅燈： 綜合判斷分數41分，較前月持平，對應的燈號仍是代表景氣熱絡的紅燈 ，連續第9個月亮出紅
燈，追平史上最長「連紅」紀錄，顯示國內經濟維持成長。國發會表示，AI需求令台廠積極擴廠，加上政府推動AI新十大建
設，民間投資有望維持動能。此外，受惠財富效應、企業調薪及就業市場穩定，民間消費有望持續成長。
• 法人看好台股年底前站上5萬點：根據歷史統計第四季台股上漲的機率接近八成 ，除了AI投資趨勢未減，年底集團作帳行情
以及美國11月期中選後不確定性下降有望支撐台股多頭情緒 。群益投顧樂觀看待台股第四季表現 ，有望上看53,000點。此
外，彭博綜合統計台灣加權指數12個月目標價達59,688點。
• 觀點：近期台股站上48,000點整數關卡，總市值達5.3兆美元，讓台股耀升成為全球第四大市值股市 ，顯示台股在AI浪潮下

的得天獨厚。年底前仍須留意中東衝突進展，若能源通膨壓力持續，聯準會升息預期恐為股市帶來波動，但考慮AI科技持續
發酵，建議投資人續抱台股，並以定期不定額的策略紀律加碼，目標追求中長期完整的AI趨勢回報。
資料來源：國發會，2026.09.29。

外匯對沖視角—美債對沖後殖利率屬全球最低之一
• 美國國債殖利率真的高嗎？在當前全球總體經濟環境下，美國國債殖利率雖維持在數十年歷史高點附近，但若單憑名目殖利
率來判斷美債的吸引力，容易忽視匯率對沖與各國財政風險帶來的溢價差異。
• 美元視角下的殖利率比較： 若將其他已開發市場的主權債務 (如德債、日債、英債)完全對沖回美元，美債在殖利率曲線上多
數期限的實際殖利率，其實與德債並列全球最低水準。相反地，日本國債與英國國債經匯率對沖回美元後的收益率反而更高。
• 跨貨幣資產交換 (ASW)的真實回報： 以10年期主權債的對沖跨貨幣資產交換計算：美國 10年期國債有效殖利率約 4.85%，
日本10年期國債(對沖至美元)有效殖利率約5.06%，德國10年期國債(對沖至美元)有效殖利率約4.51%，這表明美國政府發

債的真實對沖融資成本其實已相對精實，且符合國際市場對各國財政基本面的合理定價。
對沖至美元後的10年期主權債殖利率
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.9.29

財政基本面與信用風險—殖利率溢價反映國家債務比重
• 從歐元計價或信用風險的角度來比較 ，美債的定價邏輯非常符合總體經濟基本面： 儘管債務以歐元計價時 ，10年期美債殖
利率比同期限德國國債高出約30個基點，這反映出德國的債務佔GDP比重僅約65%，而美國則高達127%，因此美債承擔了
適度的財政溢價。儘管義大利的債務佔GDP比重高達140%且信用前景較遜色，但在統一貨幣基準下，義大利國債殖利率仍
較美國國債高出60個基點，這顯示市場並未過度壓低美債信用，美債依然享有優質避險資產的溢價優勢。
• 海外私人投資者的資金流向：近一年海外私人機構在美債加碼速度放緩，乃通膨環境下全球其他資產選擇增加，及過去升息
波段導致帳面按市值計價虧損 。然而歐洲投資人(持有一半以上的海外美債)目前仍持有龐大部位，並在等待適當的加碼時機。

• 小結與投資建議：在經過外匯對沖後，美債收益率在發達國家主權債中處於相對低位，發行成本合理且具競爭力。美債殖利
率高於德債低於義債 ，精確地反映美國政府債務負擔率 (127%)與總體經濟穩健度 。在全球公債遭遇拋售潮與通膨陰影下 ，
美債因利率期間結構與匯率優勢 ，今年以美元計價總回報優於多數 G7主權債，儘管海外私人機構投資人正在等待長端利率
落底訊號以重返市場，對美元資金或以美元計價為主的投資人而言，當前美債名目殖利率仍處於數十年高位，具備相當良好
的長期鎖利價值，但價格波動風險考量下，建議債券佈局仍以中短天期為主。
歐元計價美債殖利率高於德但低於義
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.9.29

亞洲晶⽚債券跑輸指數，評級保持穩健
• 亞洲晶⽚債券跑輸指數，評級保持穩健：儘管AI熱潮推動收⼊創下紀錄 ，但SK海力⼠和台積電的美元債 ，今年以來的表現仍
略遜於彭博亞洲美元信用債總回報指數 ，主因為大規模資本支出擴張 ，以及潛在的投⼊資本回報率下降令 其利差承壓，美國
雲服務巨頭創紀錄的 AI支出計畫加劇了壓力 ，儘管如此 SK 海力⼠和台積電仍保持較強的高評級信用實力 ，台積電計畫投
⼊600-640億美元資本支出 ，SK海力⼠的資本支出則處於 40兆韓圜區間。兩家公司穩健的資產負債表 、合計共1,260億美元
的淨現金、強勁的經營性現金流，以及在先進製程，和HBM晶⽚領域的市場領先地位，為上述支出提供支撐。
• 投資建議：現階段台積電與SK海力⼠美元債最大的問題不是信用風險 ，而是估值與利差壓力 ，短期可能因AI資本支出過大，

及市場情緒波動而表現落後指數 ，但以兩家公司強大的現金流 、淨現金部位及產業領導地位來看 ，信用品質仍屬亞洲投資級
債中的優質標的，建議利差擴大時分批布局 。

顧 • 企業獲利成長將支撐股價上行：標普500指數的長期走勢與企業每股盈餘（EPS）高度連動。高盛預估，標普500企業EPS將
於2026年達375美元，並在2027年進一步升至415美元，對應年增率約36%與11%。在利率維持高檔、金融條件偏緊的環境
下，企業獲利仍持續處於成長軌道，將為股市提供穩健的中期上行動能。
• 本益比回落提供潛在布局契機：根據FactSet數據，標普500企業第三季整體獲利預計成長30.6%。其中，資訊科技類股獲利
成長預估高達66.5%，領先能源以外的其他產業；同時，該類股預估本益比已由今年高點回落，在獲利上修與估值修正同步
發生的背景下，浮現具吸引力的中期布局機會。
• 小結：短期市場仍可能受地緣政治與利率預期擾動，但中期趨勢仍由企業獲利擴張所主導。建議投資人採取分批布局科技股

策略，聚焦具備AI受益結構、資本支出能見度高及現金流穩健的龍頭企業。

1. 本資料內容僅供內部同仁及客戶參考，非作為任何金融商品或服務之購買或銷售之推介，不得視為買賣有價證券或其他金融商品之要約或要約之引誘。
2. 本資料提及之經濟走勢預測不必然代表基金之績效，基金投資風險請詳閱基金公開說明書。
3. 基金經理公司以往之經理績效不保證基金之最低投資收益；基金經理公司除盡善良管理人之注意義務外，不負責本基金之盈虧，亦不保證最低之收益 ，投資人申購
前應詳閱基金公開說明書。
4. 本資料所載內容如涉及商品資訊 ，商品相關資訊仍以經核可之條款及文宣 (DM)為主，本行網站已備有公開說明書可供查閱基金應負擔之費用及相關投資風險 。投
資人申購基金係持有基金受益憑證，而非本資料提及之投資資產或標的。
5. 本資料所載內容取自本公司相信可靠之資料來源，且為特定日期之判斷，本公司不保證其即時性、精確性及完整性，亦不提供或嘗試遊說客戶做為投資之判斷依據。

資料內容邇後若有變更，本公司將不做預告或主動更新。投資人於決策時應審慎衡量本身風險，如因信賴本資料內容而做出或改變投資決策，須自行承擔結果，本
公司及本公司所屬員工，均不負任何損害賠償及其他法律上之責任。
6. 本資料內容之著作權屬兆豐銀行所有，禁止就所載資料任何形式之抄襲、引用或轉載。
7. 本資料所載部分基金涉及非投資等級債，投資人投資以非投資等級債券為訴求之基金不宜占其投資組合過高之比重 。基金經金管會核准，惟不表示絕無風險。由於
非投資等級債券之信用評等未達投資等級或未經信用評等，且對利率變動的敏感度甚高，故本基金可能會因利率上升、市場流動性下降，或債券發行機構違約不支
付本金、利息或破產而蒙受虧損。部分基金不適合無法承擔相關風險之投資人。基金經理公司以往之經理績效不保證基金之最低投資收益；基金經理公司除盡善良
管理人之注意義務外不負責本基金之盈虧，亦不保證最低之收益，投資人申購前應詳閱基金公開說明書。
8. 基金年化配息率計算公式：每單位配息金額 ÷除息日前一日之淨值×一年配息次數×100%。「年化配息率」為估算值。當月配息率計算公式：每單位配息金額 ÷除

息日前一日之淨值×100%。
9. 基金配息率不代表基金報酬率，且過去配息率不代表未來配息率；基金淨值可能因市場因素而上下波動。投資人因不同時間進場，將有不同之投資績效，過去之績
效亦不代表未來績效之保證。本資料如以基金過去績效進行模擬投資組合之報酬率時，僅為歷史資料模擬投資組合之結果，不代表本投資組合之實際報酬率及未來
績效保證，不同時間進行模擬操作，其結果亦可能不同。
10. 部分基金配息前未先扣除應負擔之相關費用。基金的配息可能由基金的收益或本金中支付。任何涉及由本金支出的部份，可能導致原始投資金額減損。各基金由本
金支付配息之相關資料，投資人可至各投信基金公司及總代理人網站查詢。
11. 屬基金資訊觀測站ESG基金專區所列之基金，有關基金之ESG資訊，投資人應於申購前詳閱基金公開說明書或投資人須知所載之基金所有特色或目標等資訊。

市場焦點
美國
• 9月綜合PMI升至58.4，創2021年7月以來新高 ，製造業與服務業同步加速 ，新訂單及就業亦
明顯成長 ，顯示消費 、AI投資與國防支出支撐景氣動能 。不過，需求快速擴張也加劇供應鏈
瓶頸，企業投入價格升至 2022年10月以來高點，燃料、運輸及原物料成本上升 ，顯示景氣韌
性與通膨壓力同步升溫，增加Fed政策維持緊縮的壓力。
美國
期中選舉
• 目前民調顯示 ，民主黨可能取得兩院或眾議院多數 ，這將有助於縮減未來財政赤字預期 ，進
而帶動10年期美債殖利率下滑 ，對長天期美債形成支撐 。此外既有資料顯示 ，選後一年美股
平均報酬率達12.4%，主因為政治不確定性降低，市場焦點重新回到經濟成長、企業獲利及貨
幣政策等基本面因素，進而帶動投資信心提升。

越南
• 越南股市正式納入富時羅素新興市場指數 。估計此次納入最高可引導 60億美元的資金重新流
入越南。資產管理公司Vanguard也計畫在未來幾年內，將對越南的投資額提升至約 25億美元。
• 越南股市短期仍面臨逆風 ，除了能源通膨壓力居高不下恐拖累經濟成長 ，今年以來越南成為
美國最大貿易逆差國，加上美方企圖阻止越南成為中國商品流入的中轉國 ，美對越的301條款
調查為越南出口額帶來更多不確定性。

美國景氣強勢擴張，通膨與利率壓力再升
• 企業活動創逾五年新高 ，製造業與服務業同步加速擴張 ：S&P
Global美國9月綜合PMI初值升至58.4，創2021年7月以來新高，
且連續四個月加速成長；服務業 PMI升至58.7（59個月高點）、
製造業PMI升至57.0（52個月高點），產出亦同步加速 ，加上
訂單與就業成長皆達近年最快 ，顯示內需、AI投資及國防支出
共同支撐企業活動。S&P Global依PMI調查推估經濟年成長約
5%，Q3則上看4%，反映短期景氣動能明顯增強。
• 供給受限推高成本 ，通膨壓力再升溫： 需求快速擴張同時推高
供應鏈與產能壓力 ，企業未完成訂單升至 2022年5月以來高點 ，
供應商交貨延遲亦明顯惡化 ，反映產能不足 ，投入價格指數升

至2022年10月以來新高 ，企業主要反映燃料 、運輸、原物料
及薪資成本上升 ，顯示供給受限與能源成本可能持續向終端價
格傳導，增加通膨黏著風險並提高Fed維持緊縮政策的壓力。
• 後市觀察：數據公布後，10年期美債殖利率一度升至約 5.12%，
創2007年以來新高 ，市場對10月再升息的押注亦升溫 ，短線
需關注供應鏈壓力是否緩解 ，以及後續ISM、就業與物價數據
能否確認通膨再升溫趨勢 。投資策略建議關注利率敏感度較低
的高品質成長股或防禦型資產 ，可適度布局受惠強勁內需與 AI
／國防支出的美股大型科技與工業類股 ，並留意升息環境下金
融股的利差受益機會 ，整體建議維持分散配置並嚴控利率風險 。
資料來源：S&P Global PMI, Bureau of Economic Analysis via S&P Global Market
Intelligence， 2026.9.24

能源成本升高推升通膨，利率維持高檔壓力升溫
• 能源與運輸成本上升，增加通膨黏著風險：中東衝突與
俄羅斯煉油設施遭攻擊 ，使全球柴油供應持續偏緊 ，燃
料價格及運輸成本同步上升；由於柴油廣泛應用於運輸 、
農業與製造業，價格上漲將透過物流及生產成本向下游
傳導。目前全球柴油庫存偏低 、煉油產能受限，若供應
中斷延續，可能使能源價格維持高檔並延長通膨壓力。
• 公債與企業債供給增加 ，長端利率壓力升高：美國財政
赤字及政府融資需求偏高 ，加上大型科技公司持續為 AI
資料中心等基礎建設籌資 ，債券供給增加使市場承接壓
力升高；在通膨預期未完全穩定的情況下 ，投資人對長
天期債券要求較高的期限溢酬 ，進一步推升長端殖利率 ，
同時AI相關企業債近期亦出現利差擴大 ，反映市場對大

量發債及資本支出回收期的關注升高。
• 資產配置面臨成長與通膨雙重拉扯： 能源價格、企業融
資成本及長端殖利率若持續處於高檔 ，將提高企業資金
成本並壓縮高估值資產的評價空間；同時 ，利率與能源
價格波動也可能擴大股 、債市震盪。因此資產配置建議
降低長天期債券對利率變化的敏感度 ，提高對通膨與利
率波動的防禦能力 ，並以現金流穩定、具備定價能力之
優質標的為核心。
資料來源：Federal Reserve Bank of St. Louis via FRED ， 2026.9.8

美國期中選舉-目前民調顯示民主黨可能取得兩院或眾議院多數
• 目前民調顯示民主黨可能取得兩院或眾議院多數 ：最新民調顯示，民主黨
於11月美國期中選舉，取得國會多數席次的機率已領先共和黨，根據市場
情境分析，期中選舉不影響既有法案執行 ，但將左右未來兩年的財政預算 、
稅制改革 、政府支出及國會監督權限 。情境(1)若共和黨續掌國會 ，減稅
政策延長、產業回流、關稅及移民限制等議程有望加速推進 ，財政擴張力
道可能進一步增強 。情境(2)分裂國會 ，兩黨制衡將使重大法案推動難度
提高，新增減稅與大型財政刺激方案較難落地 。情境(3)民主黨全面掌控
國會，則可能提高對政府政策的監督力度 ，政策方向偏向提高稅負與監管 ，
壓抑企業獲利 ，使政策環境趨向平衡。
美國期中選舉結果預估

美國期中選舉-對於股債市的影響
• 對股市的影響：美國銀行分析1900至2025年共31次美國期中選舉，資料發現標普500在期中選舉前12個月平均報酬約為2.9%，
低於所有年度平均 8.9%。因為市場在選舉前往往受到政策不確定性 、政黨競爭及投資人觀望情緒影響 ，波動相對較大 。然而
此現象不代表選舉必然導致市場疲弱，因為當時的經濟環境、通膨、利率及企業獲利等因素同樣扮演重要角色。期中選舉後12
個月的股市表現通常較佳 。歷史數據顯示，選後一年美股平均報酬率達12.4%。主因為政治不確定性降低，市場焦點重新回到
經濟成長、企業獲利及貨幣政策等基本面因素，進而帶動投資信心提升。
• 對債市的影響：若民主黨取得國會主導權，市場普遍預期財政支出擴張幅度將較受節制 ，有助於縮減未來財政赤字預期，進而

帶動10年期美債殖利率下滑，對長天期美債形成支撐，若共和黨持續全面掌控國會，市場可能預期減稅及財政擴張政策延續，
進一步推升財政赤字預估，帶動公債殖利率走高，對長天期美債形成壓力，不過市場已部分反映相關預期，實際衝擊幅度有限。
美國期中選舉前後對於美股的影響
資料來源：美國銀行，2026.7

AI建設熱潮吞噬自由現金流
• 資本支出創新高：雲端服務巨頭(包括亞馬遜、Alphabet、微軟、Meta、甲骨文以及SpaceX等)正在全力擴充AI算力與資料
中心，2026年大型雲端服務巨頭的資本支出合計預估將接近 9,600億美元，而2025至2031年的累計AI資本支出更可能逼近
9兆美元。
• 自由現金流出現赤字： 高額的投資加上股東回報(股息與庫藏股買回)，使許多巨頭的自由現金流(FCF)轉為負值(如Alphabet、
亞馬遜、微軟、Meta及甲骨文等)，預計到2027年，部分企業將更加依賴外部融資，而非單靠內部現金生成。
• 債券發行量大增： 為了填補資金缺口 ，科技業債券發行規模急遽上升 ，2026年迄今，各幣別的投資級科技債券發行量已突
破4,000億美元，創下歷史新高。

獲利韌性與金庫深度：強勁EBITDA成為信用安全網
• 上兆美元的流動性護城河： 儘管債務增加，但科技十二巨頭合計持有近 1兆美元的等值現金，整體科技板塊流動性充裕 ，短
期內無流動性危機。
• EBITDA強勁成長消化債務：市場預期覆蓋的12家最大科技企業在2026與2027年的EBITDA合計將成長約35%，強勁的獲利
能力顯著降低槓桿比率的上升壓力，確保信評機構維護穩定的信用評級。
• 資本配置策略分化：(1)兼顧股東回報：蘋果、微軟、Meta與Alphabet在投入AI同時，仍維持大規模的庫藏股買回與股息發
放。(2)全數投入資本支出：亞馬遜將絕大部分現金導向資本支出(2026年資本支出預計達2,200億美元居首)，且不發放股息。
• 小結與投資建議：科技巨頭為維持AI技術與基礎設施領先優勢，短期內，負自由現金流將成為常態。然而，這並非財務危機

訊號，而是企業利用超強獲利能力 (高EBITDA)與龐大的現金儲備進行戰略性擴張 ，科技板塊的信用風險目前處於可控且健
康的狀態，投資建議聚焦AI變現能力與EBITDA成長，優先佈局具有強大獲利護城河且能將算力轉化為實質營收的龍頭企業 。

越南股市正式納入富時羅素新興市場指數
• 預估引導60億美元資金流入越南： 富時羅素估計，此次納入最高可引導 60億美元的資金重新流入越南 。受此提振，資產管
理公司Vanguard也計畫在未來幾年內，將對越南的投資額提升至約25億美元。整個指數過渡將分為四個階段推行至2027年：
今年9月率先納入10%，明年3月追加20%，最後於明年6月與9月分別完成剩餘各35%的分配。市場分析指出，隨著明年3月
配額加大，市場關注度與對本地投資人的影響將更為顯著。
• 越南股市短期仍面臨逆風： 儘管官方早前誓言今年 GDP成長目標達10%，但中東衝突以來能源通膨壓力居高不下恐拖累經
濟成長。此外，越南與美國的貿易協商仍在進行中，由於今年以來越南成為美國最大貿易逆差國，加上美方企圖阻止越南成

為中國商品流入的中轉國，美對越的301條款調查為越南出口額帶來更多不確定性。
• 觀點：儘管短線面臨不確定性 ，但越南股市同時具備多項利多題材 ，包含企業獲利成長、公共投資加速、FDI回流及資本市
場改革等。中長期結構仍偏正向，可以衛星配置的角度布局，或是以定期定額的方式參與長期成長潛力。
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.09。

2. 本資料提及之經濟走勢預測不必然代表基金之績效，基金投資風險請詳閱基金公開說明書。
3. 基金經理公司以往之經理績效不保證基金之最低投資收益；基金經理公司除盡善良管理人之注意義務外，不負責本基金之盈虧，亦不保證最低之收益 ，投資人申購
前應詳閱基金公開說明書。
4. 本資料所載內容如涉及商品資訊 ，商品相關資訊仍以經核可之條款及文宣 (DM)為主，本行網站已備有公開說明書可供查閱基金應負擔之費用及相關投資風險 。投
資人申購基金係持有基金受益憑證，而非本資料提及之投資資產或標的。
5. 本資料所載內容取自本公司相信可靠之資料來源，且為特定日期之判斷，本公司不保證其即時性、精確性及完整性，亦不提供或嘗試遊說客戶做為投資之判斷依據。

資料內容邇後若有變更，本公司將不做預告或主動更新。投資人於決策時應審慎衡量本身風險，如因信賴本資料內容而做出或改變投資決策，須自行承擔結果，本
公司及本公司所屬員工，均不負任何損害賠償及其他法律上之責任。
6. 本資料內容之著作權屬兆豐銀行所有，禁止就所載資料任何形式之抄襲、引用或轉載。
7. 本資料所載部分基金涉及非投資等級債，投資人投資以非投資等級債券為訴求之基金不宜占其投資組合過高之比重 。基金經金管會核准，惟不表示絕無風險。由於
非投資等級債券之信用評等未達投資等級或未經信用評等，且對利率變動的敏感度甚高，故本基金可能會因利率上升、市場流動性下降，或債券發行機構違約不支
付本金、利息或破產而蒙受虧損。部分基金不適合無法承擔相關風險之投資人。基金經理公司以往之經理績效不保證基金之最低投資收益；基金經理公司除盡善良
管理人之注意義務外不負責本基金之盈虧，亦不保證最低之收益，投資人申購前應詳閱基金公開說明書。
8. 基金年化配息率計算公式：每單位配息金額 ÷除息日前一日之淨值×一年配息次數×100%。「年化配息率」為估算值。當月配息率計算公式：每單位配息金額 ÷除

息日前一日之淨值×100%。
9. 基金配息率不代表基金報酬率，且過去配息率不代表未來配息率；基金淨值可能因市場因素而上下波動。投資人因不同時間進場，將有不同之投資績效，過去之績
效亦不代表未來績效之保證。本資料如以基金過去績效進行模擬投資組合之報酬率時，僅為歷史資料模擬投資組合之結果，不代表本投資組合之實際報酬率及未來
績效保證，不同時間進行模擬操作，其結果亦可能不同。
10. 部分基金配息前未先扣除應負擔之相關費用。基金的配息可能由基金的收益或本金中支付。任何涉及由本金支出的部份，可能導致原始投資金額減損。各基金由本
金支付配息之相關資料，投資人可至各投信基金公司及總代理人網站查詢。
11. 屬基金資訊觀測站ESG基金專區所列之基金，有關基金之ESG資訊，投資人應於申購前詳閱基金公開說明書或投資人須知所載之基金所有特色或目標等資訊。

during Aug, resulting in 3Q26 revenues up 74% YoY to NT$843m. With the gradual ramp 
up of clients’ new platform, we expect to see sequential MoM growth ahead, with Oct/

Earnings revision: We factor in UMT 3Q26 revenues and revise down earnings by 1%/

delivery, while we remain positive on the rising LEO satellite business exposure ahead.

Valuation: We continue to derive our target price based on a discounted P/E, and based 
on 2029E (methodology unchanged). We continue to derive the target P/E multiple from 
the average PEG&M ratio of peers in the satellite supply chain, and we remove outliers 
(telecom operators who face competition from LEO satellite operators). Our target 
2029E discounted P/E multiple is at 37.1x (vs. previously at 37.0x) which is based on 
peers’ avg. PEG&M, and UMT’s 2029-30E avg. NI YoY and OPM, due to higher avg. OPM 
in 2029-30E at 42% (vs. previously at 41%). We apply the 37.1x multiple to 2029E EPS 
and discount it back to 2027E via a COE of 7.8% (no change). Our 12m target price is at 
NT$2,549 (vs. previously at NT$2,557), implying 61x 2027E P/E, which is above the 
company’s average +1 stv. P/E of 40x, reflecting our positive view on UMT’s product mix 
upgrade toward LEO satellites. Maintain Buy.

### Page 4

Price target risks and methodology - UMT 
Valuation: We use a discounted P/E methodology and apply a 37.1x target P/E multiple 
to UMT’s 2029E EPS, discounting it back to 2027E at a COE of 7.8% (beta 1.2x, risk-free 
rate 1.6% and market risk premium at 5.1%), which leads to our 12-month target price 
of NT$2,549. The target P/E is based on the average PEG&M ratio of peers in the 
satellite supply chain. We are Buy-rated on UMT.  
Key Risks: Slower-than-expected LEO satellite deployment; Potential competition from 
new-entrant suppliers; LEO satellite operators manufacturing in-house components.

Ratings, coverage universe and related definitions
Buy (B), Neutral (N), Sell (S) Analysts recommend stocks as Buys or Sells for inclusion on various regional Investment Lists. Being assigned a Buy or Sell
on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a Buy or a Sell on an
Investment List with an active rating (i.e., a  stock that is not Rating Suspended, Not Rated, Early-Stage Biotech, Coverage Suspended or Not Covered), is

deemed Neutral. Each region manages Regional Conviction Lists, which are selected from Buy rated stocks on the respective region’s Investment Lists
and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

Total return potential represents the upside or downside differential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all covered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.
Coverage Universe: A list of all stocks in each coverage universe is available by primary analyst, stock and coverage universe at

https://www.gs.com/research/hedge.html.

Goldman Sachs is acting in an advisory capacity in a merger or in a strategic transaction involving this company, when there are legal, regulatory or
policy constraints due to Goldman Sachs’ involvement in a transaction, and in certain other circumstances. Early-Stage Biotech (ES). An investment

rating and a target price are not assigned pursuant to Goldman Sachs policy when this company has neither a drug, treatment or medical device that

investment rating or target price. The previous investment rating and target price, if any, are no longer in effect for this stock and should not be relied

upon.  Coverage Suspended (CS). Goldman Sachs has suspended coverage of this company.  Not Covered (NC). Goldman Sachs does not cover this
company.

### Page 5

Server TAM estimate revisions

AI server racks: We raise our forecast for AI server racks powered by NVIDIA to 61k / 
97k / 149k in 2026 - 28E (vs. 50k / 92k / 148k previously), reflecting the increase in our 
forecasts for brand makers (Dell, SuperMicro, HP), leading to higher US CSP capex by 4% 
/ 17% / 22% in 2026-28E, and NVIDIA chipset shipments (raised by 9% / 15% in 2027 / 
28E), showing the ongoing upcycle of AI infrastructure. Overall, we expect the AI server 
rack global value TAM to grow at 163% CAGR in 2025-28E to US$1tn, or 63% of the 
global server value TAM by 2028E. By vendor, we continue to expect global leading CSPs 
to be the major customers, with leading ODM direct players as the major suppliers. We 
expect Hon Hai / FII to maintain the leading market position among ODMs and with 
continuous specification upgrade driving its global market share expansion.

AI servers: We raise AI server (8-GPU) equivalent shipment by 10% / 29% in 2027-28E 
to 2.7m / 3.3m in 2027-28E, mainly driven by rising ASIC-based AI server demand. The 
custom-design provides ASIC-based AI servers optimized for AI computation with better 
efficiency, such as lower latency, or memory savings via reduced expensive read/write 
cycles. We continue to see new generation ASICs launched by global leading CSPs, 
supporting AI server growth: we expect the AI servers (8-GPU) global value TAM to grow

### Page 6

at 38% CAGR in 2025-28E to US$318bn, or 20% of global server value TAM by 2028E. 
We reduce 2026E shipment by 7% to 1.7m, reflecting a shift toward higher density AI 
server racks driven by leading US CSPs.

General servers: We raise general server revenue by 15% / 13% / 11% in 2026-28E 
driven by high memory costs and ongoing specification upgrades driving AI workloads. 
Intel launched its Intel Xeon 6+ processors at Computex 2026 (link) to facilitate Agentic 
AI workloads, positioning: CPU servers as the control plane, driving system-level 
performance and efficiency under rising agentic AI workloads, while supporting data 
movement and sustained inferencing. Intel presented Xeon 7 Diamond Rapids in Aug 
2026 (link), with architectures for Agentic AI and Enterprise scale workloads, 
empowering efficient inference and edge computing. AMD has begun production ramp 
of its 6th Gen AMD EPYC CPUs (Venice) in May (link), which powers agentic AI and 
diverse workloads - running the agents, AI host nodes, and general purposes that turns 
intelligence into action with optimized TCO (total cost of ownership). We expect general 
servers’ global value TAM to grow at 22% CAGR in 2025-28E to US$271bn, or 17% of 
global server value TAM by 2028E.

CSP capex: Regarding customer spending, our US internet team forecasts that leading 
US cloud service providers (CSPs) in aggregate will grow their capex budgets by 84%/ 
52%/ 13% YoY in 2026-28E, with our estimates being raised by 4% / 17% / 22% in 
2026-28E to US$1,397bn by 2028E. Within the China internet space, we expect leading

### Page 7

Chinese cloud platforms to grow capex by 105%/ 21%/ 14% YoY in 2026-28E, with our 
estimates being raised by 14% / 15% / 11% in 2026-28E. The strong increase in CSP 
capex reflects the high memory costs and growing demand on AI workloads. In terms of 
capex structure, we estimate chipsets (GPU, ASIC, CPU) remain the major contributors, 
along with memory (HBM), and followed by networking and other components (e.g. 
cooling, power, chassis, rail kits, etc.). Compared to global CSPs, we estimate China CSP 
spending on chipsets would be more diversified across GPU and ASICs considering 
various local fabless suppliers and customers’ varying demand on AI scenarios / tasks. 
Read more: Microsoft, Amazon, Meta, Alphabet, Oracle, Baba, Tencent, Baidu.  
Key highlights on the recent Maia 300 announcement from MSFT: (1) Microsoft to 
expand its Azure Maia system to multi-gigawatt deployments, making Maia the primary 
inference system for Microsoft products and services, and (2) Microsoft plans to have 
multi-gigawatt Maia deployment beginning in 2027, with reports indicating a potential 
plan to deploy >1M Maia 300 chips in 2027 (Microsoft is covered by Gabriela Borges).

Ratings, coverage universe and related definitions

on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a Buy or a Sell on an
Investment List with an active rating (i.e., a  stock that is not Rating Suspended, Not Rated, Early-Stage Biotech, Coverage Suspended or Not Covered), is

deemed Neutral. Each region manages Regional Conviction Lists, which are selected from Buy rated stocks on the respective region’s Investment Lists
and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or

營收走勢圖

研究員/游周翰 Jeffery.Yu@uni-psg.com

研究部重申華星光買進建議，主要考量：(1)管理層維持下半年營運優
於上半年展望，受惠 800G DCI 及 CW Laser 需求持續成長，4Q26 單月
營收有望挑戰 6 億元；(2)隨晶粒稼動率及良率持續改善，研究部上修
2027 年晶粒出貨量預估，加上 EML 等產品逐步放量，有望帶動產品組
合優化及獲利能力提升。綜上所述，研究部預估華星光 2026/2027 年
營收，由原先 60.6 億元/119.8 億元調整為 58.4 億元/120.8 億元
(+33%/107%YoY)，EPS 由原先 7.69 元/15.88 元調整為 7.5 元/16.35
元。考量2027 年產能擴充及相關產品出貨成長，營收與獲利有望呈現
倍數成長，維持目標價 800 元的買進建議。

重點摘要： 
1. 3Q26營收優於研究部預期，惟受匯率影響EPS僅符合預期： 
華星光於 10/14 公 佈 自 結 ，9 月營收 5.75 億元（ +5.3%MoM 、
+97.8%YoY） ，稅後淨利1.03億元，稅後淨利率達17.9%，EPS達
0.72元。相較於8月回升，除了匯率回穩外，亦受到營收規模放
大所影響 。 累計3Q26營收為16.29億元 （+26.4%QoQ，+51.4%YoY），
稅後淨利達3.26億元，EPS為2.28元，創歷史新高。若依研究部
過去費用率及業外假設回推，3Q26毛利率約28-29%（約+6ppts 
QoQ） ，主要受惠先前財稅認列時間差回補、產能利用率提升及
產品組合優化 。 整體而言 ，3Q26營收優於研究部預期16.19億元 ，
EPS僅符合預期2.26元 ， 推測主因受到匯率所影響毛利率及業外
損益。

表一、3Q26 研究部預估與實際差異 
單位：百萬元 實際 研究部原估 差異

### Page 2

請參見末頁重要批露及免責聲明  2

2. 下半年營運優於上半年，惟毛利率受新產品良率等因素影響 ： 
展望4Q26， 管理層維持2H26營運優於1H26， 除了既有DCI產品持
續成長外，CW Laser需求持續提升、800G DCI進入批量生產，
以及EML與高增益APD開始貢獻營收，有望帶動整體營運向上，
單月營收有望挑戰6億元 。 近期經研究部通路訪查 ，800GZR出貨
量略低於原先預估，因此研究部下修 4Q26出貨量從75K下修至
55K，4Q26營收從原先預估19.6億元下修至17.31億元。此外考
量800GZR毛利率因良率及協助客戶購買稀釋毛利率不如原先研
究部預期的情況下，將EPS從2.51元下修至2.29元。

3. 隨產能擴充，營收獲利將持續成長： 
展望未來，在2027年產能倍增情況下，營收規模有望呈現倍數
成長。晶粒方面，除了既有的CW Laser產能擴充外，亦增加EML
晶粒產能，使公司在InP產品線有多種產品分散風險。此外，近
期經供應鏈訪查，晶粒稼動率及良率提升下，有望使整體晶粒
出貨量優於原先預期。因此研究部預估2027年整體晶粒出貨量
從4700萬顆上修至5000萬顆。綜上所述，研究部預估營收2027
年營收從119.8億元上修至120.8億元 ， EPS從15.88元微幅上修
至16.35元。

## Document 254: Capital Market Outlook (2026-09-14)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Capital Market Outlook (2026-09-14).pdf
Report source: Merrill Lynch (CMO)
Date: 2026-09-14
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

CHIEF INVESTMENT OFFICE
Capital Market Outlook
September 14, 2026
All data, projections and opinions are as of the date of this report and subject to change.
IN THIS ISSUE
Macro Strategy—Superintelligence and Superabundance in the Next Decade:
Rapid advances in Artificial Intelligence (AI) have prompted prominent architects of
the new economy to conclude the Singularity is arriving ahead of schedule as signs
of its effects proliferate in the multitude of new AI models running in the increasing

number of data centers driving one of the biggest investment booms in U.S. history.
Superintelligence that surpasses collective human intelligence has long been one of the
predictions of futurists analyzing the implications of the Singularity. Superabundance is
one of the results of Superintelligence predicted by those who have written about the
consequences of this major acceleration in technological innovation. The early arrival of
these radical forces for change poses imminent challenges for society and investors that

will shape the next decade in critical ways.
In particular, the heightened uncertainty about how this new era plays out increases the
utility of a well-diversified approach to wealth management in our view.
Market View—Elevated Debt Supply Is Testing the Market's Appetite: The recent
rise in Treasury yields reflects a confluence of factors, including Federal Reserve (Fed)
policy uncertainty, deteriorating fiscal dynamics, resilient economic growth, persistent

inflation concerns and a record wave of debt issuance from both the U.S. Treasury and
corporate borrowers, particularly in the long end of the curve. Importantly, we view this
as a period of market adjustment rather than a cause for alarm.
Episodes of debt pressures can be a natural consequence of elevated supply, in addition
to other external factors and could be resolved through higher yields that help balance
supply and demand. While interest rate volatility may remain elevated and long-term

yields could settle at structurally higher levels, today's combination of attractive nominal
and real yields presents a potential opportunity for investors to extend beyond cash and
short-duration holdings and move closer to strategic duration targets.
The Ticker Tape—Top Questions Answered Directly by Our Chief Investment
Officer (CIO).
• What are the markets actually telling us?
AUTHORS
Chief Investment Office
Macro Strategy Team
Chief Investment Office

Fixed Income Strategy Team
Christopher Hyzy
Chief Investment Officer

Portfolio Considerations
We view recent volatility as a rolling
correction rather than the end of
the cycle. We favor diversified Equity
exposure, with potential opportunities
extending beyond Growth to Value,
Small- and Mid-caps, Non-U.S. Equities
(especially Emerging Markets), dividend-
focused and thematic strategies.

We favor adjusting portfolios toward
strategic durations given attractive
elevated nominal and real yields, and see
value in high-quality bonds, particularly
longer-dated municipal bonds.
Consider broader portfolio
diversification with potential
opportunities across industrial metals,
energy, Real/Tangible Assets, Hedge
Funds, private markets and global
strategies to capture themes from AI,
reindustrialization, infrastructure growth
and market dispersion.

### Page 2

2 of 8 September 14, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MACRO STRATEGY
Superintelligence and Superabundance in the Next Decade
Chief Investment Office, Macro Strategy Team
While some academics have claimed the Singularity is still off in the distant future, or even
unlikely at all, those on the front lines of AI model development have recently asserted that it
has already begun. For example, Elon Musk has pointed to recent issues with AI models going

outside their proscribed limits, including hacking external systems and completing previously
unsolved math problems. Over a year ago, Sam Altman wrote, "We are past the event horizon; the
takeoff has started. Humanity is close to building superintelligence..." In short, the rapid evolution
of these models has convinced knowledgeable observers that the Singularity has begun.
Superintelligence is the primary driver of the Singularity as exponential advances in AI ultimately

create an artificial Superintelligence that is much smarter and faster than the collective
intelligence of humanity. As such, it can solve practical problems that remain unsolved by humans
and do it in a timely fashion. Superabundance is the logical implication of Superintelligence
as it works to create a world where economic goods, services and energy are essentially free
because of the omniscience implied by the most efficient processing of unlimited information by

superintelligent models. In essence, Superintelligence could mimic the Creation.
Some aspects of Superabundance include automated production of desired output that
eliminates most physical labor, clean energy and synthetic materials that eliminate resource
scarcity, and increased longevity as Superintelligence is applied to maintaining physical health
via, for example, synthetic organ replacement and personalized illness treatments. Obviously,
Superabundance pulls the rug out from under traditional economics, which is the study of how

scarce resources are allocated. While the "scarcity" vanishes in this new world, the allocation issue
remains and is likely to become the focus of political debate. In particular, the question of how
jobs and incomes fit into a superabundant world is likely to become a bigger and bigger issue.
The trend of technology replacing more and more of the labor share of income is already decades
old and dates back to the beginnings of digital technology. That trend has picked up speed

with the increasing profit share of output the new technologies created. The ratio of income
to the number of workers is orders of magnitude higher for the big new technology companies
compared to the old economy companies of the past. This means the owners of capital
(shareholders) are receiving a bigger share of the economic pie as high-margin, high-productivity
companies based in the new technology become a bigger part of the economy. This trend is

accelerating. That is a direct implication of the movement toward and through the Singularity.
It means the issue of income inequality, which has always been on society's radar screen, will
become bigger in the years ahead unless there are wise policies to share the superabundance
of the near future. Some countries are better prepared for this future than others. Norway,
for example, has built a sovereign wealth fund (SWF) over the past 25 years that represents a

substantial share of capital income for each of its citizens. In the U.S., with its widespread equity
culture, thanks to Individual Retirement Accounts and 401K plans, capital income is more widely
distributed compared to Europe with its much more debt-based capital markets. In any event,
widespread equity ownership is one way to deal with a superabundant economy where AI has
significantly reduced the labor share of income.
Demographics add to the natural evolution from labor income to capital income as societies

become wealthier. Aging societies of retirees by definition depend on capital income more than
labor income. This transition of the economy to higher margins and faster earnings growth is
the fortunate accompaniment to the need for more income from capital as societies become
older. The longevity trend naturally accelerates this process, which is the logical result of
technological progress raising productivity and living standards. People live longer in wealthy

societies, as subsistence constraints on life spans diminish while lifestyle and mental health
constraints become more the issue. How lifestyles and mental health evolve in a world where
work becomes less necessary is already showing signs of their increased importance for the
future of public health.
The current accelerating profits cycle reflects this impact of new technology on the relative
returns to labor and capital. The trend so far has been relatively slow compared to what's

coming if Superintelligence and Superabundance are the outcomes the Singularity promises as
increasingly seems likely. Because much financial analysis relies on past cycles the accelerating
Investment Implications

profits and rising margins of the current economy have naturally focused attention on what
happens when conditions revert to the mean of lower margins and more historically normal
profit growth. Looked at through this lens, Equities appear overvalued and destined to correct
bubble-like conditions. The Singularity, however, implies "this time is different," the four most
dangerous words in investing. Yet, the slowly evolving shift toward capital income already under

way is an early warning sign of bigger things to come as these trends are turbo-charged to warp
speed while the economy passes through the Singularity. Seen this way, a super-cycle in profits,
margins and stock values seems logical.
A revamping of the social safety net to address the conditions implied by Superabundance
is already a hot topic in Silicon Valley. Various proposals for a universal basic income (UBI)
are being bandied about. Typically, UBI refers to the government giving everyone an income

sufficient to meet some living standard. This automatically implies a big role for government in
the new economy. Given the fourth-turning breakdown in the political process and governance,
alternatives that avoid the government sector might also be considered. For example, another
implication of the Singularity is an accelerating accumulation of wealth fueling a growing supply
of altruistic and philanthropic potential that arguably could be administered more efficiently by

the private sector than the government where widespread allegations of corrupt administration
at all levels have proliferated since the pandemic. Given the growing wealth pie and the diversity
of social priorities among its owners addressing more social needs through philanthropy is a
likely option for the superabundant economy. In any event, the relative roles of government and
the private sector are necessarily an issue to be hashed out in this revolutionary time.

More glimmers of the UBI-like movements have popped up recently. As mentioned, Norway's
SWF is an example of how a well-administered government has been preparing for this future.
In the U.S. government sponsored tax-advantaged savings accounts from birth have been
instituted with private donations being added to the program. Governments, including the U.S.,
have increasingly been buying stakes in private investments for various purposes with the result

that the focus solely on sovereign debt can be a misleading indicator of fiscal fragility.
Japan is a significant example of the difference between gross government debt and net
government debt after subtracting government owned assets. Its gross debt is around 279% of
its gross domestic product (GDP) which is much higher than any other major economy. However,
Japan government assets total around 192% of GDP , including domestic and foreign Equities,

bonds, and loans. As a result, its net debt is less than 100% of GDP and more in line with other
major economies. As one of the most aged societies in the world, Japan points the way toward
how societies can cope with longevity. Government ownership of a broad array of higher yielding
global assets funded with low sovereign debt interest rates appears to be a path the U.S.
Treasury is also starting to pursue in managing its debt burden.
The main risks to the uptopian promise of Superabundance are the dystopian possibilities

that would follow from poor social decision-making. Around the world, different governments
will follow different approaches to navigating the unprecedented challenges posed by the
Singularity. As the U.S. celebrates its 250-year anniversary, it is in the midst of its fourth "fourth
turning." These are the intense political upheaval periods that demographers and historians have
documented in every fourth generation. The Revolutionary War, the Civil War, and the Great

Depression and World War II mark the first three "fourth turnings." The Singularity is a major
marker for the current fourth turning.
The relative success of the U.S. in transitioning through the previous major turning points in
history provides hope for a utopian rather than a dystopian outcome. Other countries have not
transited these challenges so well for such an extended period which helps explain how the
U.S. has become the most prosperous country on earth. Nevertheless, past performance is no

guarantee of future success. As always, each generation faces new challenges to overcome, or not.
For investors, the Singularity seems to be behind an unusually strong earnings cycle that could
potentially last much longer than past history would suggest. That's positive for Equities and
risk assets in general. The extra risks associated with this rapid transformation argue for well
diversified holdings as creative destruction on steroids seems a plausible outcome in a world of

warp speed change.

MARKET VIEW
Elevated Debt Supply Is Testing the Market's Appetite
Chief Investment Office, Fixed Income Strategy Team
For much of the past two decades, rising debt levels appeared to carry few
consequences. Federal deficits expanded and corporate borrowing increased, yet
Treasury yields generally trended lower and credit spreads stayed remarkably tame.
Today, the backdrop may be changing.

Federal borrowing needs continue to grow as deficit spending remains elevated. Net
interest expense has surpassed $1 trillion annually and now rivals total U.S. defense
spending. On this front there appears no end in sight as the Congressional Budget
Office projects that the fiscal 2026 shortfall will widen to roughly $2.1 trillion. Investors
are increasingly focused on not only the level of debt outstanding, but also the pace at
which new debt is being issued—with some questioning about the sustainability of this

situation over the longer term.
This phenomenon is not limited to Treasurys, as the issuance glut extends to corporate
debt. Investment-grade (IG) corporate issuance is running roughly 40% above last year
and is on pace for a record year. 1 While demand for Fixed Income remains healthy, the
sheer volume of new debt being brought to market is beginning to test investor capacity,
particularly at the long end of the Treasury curve and within certain sectors.

Hyperscalers Are Competing for Long-Duration Capital. A significant driver of
the surge in corporate issuance has been the hyperscalers. The largest technology
companies are financing an unprecedented wave of AI-related capital spending, leading
to nearly $280 billion of debt issuance year-to-date. 2 Many of these issuers carry high
credit ratings and have ramped up issuance of longer-maturity bonds. As a result, they
are increasingly competing with long-dated Treasurys for the same pool of institutional

capital, including insurance companies, pension funds, sovereign wealth funds and
duration-oriented Fixed Income investors—creating a crowding-out effect amongst high-
quality Fixed Income.
Fortunately, this is a case of pressure, not a credit crisis. Recent weakness in long-dated
Treasurys reflects a classic supply-and-demand imbalance and importantly is not a
solvency crisis for the U.S. government or high-quality hyperscalers. Investors are being

asked to absorb a growing volume of net new issuance from both the U.S. Treasury and
corporate America at the same time.
Debt Pressure and a Normalization, Not a Fiscal Reckoning. The rise in long-end
yields during August reflects this dynamic. The 30-year Treasury yield moved above
5.3%, reaching levels not seen since 2007. Fiscal concerns likely played a role, but we

### Page 5

5 of 8 September 14, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
believe heavy supply, sticky inflation, reduced Fed demand and increased competition for
investor capital were equally important contributors.
And importantly, the market's adjustment mechanism is already working. Higher Treasury
yields and somewhat wider credit spreads are attracting buyers and helping clear the growing
supply of bonds. This is a natural normalization and is how markets restore equilibrium.

Recent Treasury auctions continue to attract demand consistent with historical norms, and
investor appetite for new IG issuance has remained robust. These are not characteristics
typically associated with a loss of confidence in U.S., or corporate, creditworthiness.
A New Yield Regime. For Fixed Income investors, the more important implication
is that a 4% to 5% 10-year Treasury yield may represent the new normal. In an
environment of very strong underlying economic growth and unprecedented volumes of

debt issuance from both the public and private sectors, higher yields may be required to
attract sufficient capital amid a more competitive landscape for yield.
We continue to view both nominal and real yields as attractive and believe investors
should remain focused on achieving strategic duration targets. While rate volatility may
persist, today's yield levels provide a potential opportunity to seek attractive income that
has been largely absent for much of the past decade.

The Bigger Picture: Growth Still Matters More Than Debt Levels. None of this dismisses
the long-term fiscal challenges facing the U.S. Debt levels are rising, interest costs
continue to grow, and mandatory spending increasingly consumes a larger share of the
federal budget. However, history shows that debt levels alone do not determine interest
rates. Between 2007 and 2020, U.S. debt rose dramatically even as the 10-year Treasury
yield fell from roughly 5% to below 1%. What ultimately matters is the interplay between

short-term interest rates, debt levels, economic growth, inflation and investor demand.
For instance, nominal GDP growth and the 10-year yield tend to be correlated, outside
of recessions—we believe recent rate increases partly reflect this strong macro
backdrop. Moreover, economic growth is running above long-end yields, supporting the
sustainability of rising government debt.

The U.S. continues to benefit from several structural advantages: the dollar remains
the world's reserve currency, Treasurys remain the foundation of the global financial

system, and U.S. capital markets are still the deepest and most liquid in the world. Those
advantages continue to support demand for U.S. assets and underpin sovereign credit
quality even as borrowing needs grow.
The bottom line, in our view, is that even though the recent rise in long-end yields catches
many headlines, there is no need to panic. Instead, the recent repricing reflects periodic
episodes of debt pressure rather than a fundamental loss of confidence in U.S. credit. With

borrowing demand from both Washington and corporate America likely to remain elevated,
bond prices may continue to serve as the market's release valve. The consequence is likely
a world of structurally higher long-term yields, not a sovereign debt crisis.

6 of 8 September 14, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
THE TICKER TAPE
What are the markets actually telling us?
Christopher Hyzy, Chief Investmen t Officer
When the markets speak loudly we should listen. There are often clues in the internal trends
within the markets that help us determine how far into an economic cycle we are or what drivers
of growth are most impactful. In addition, we can also find clues that help with determining

the micro picture. In other words, what industries are the most likely to benefit, from a
profitability standpoint is concerned, as the growth picture unfolds? Currently, the markets have
a megaphone, in our view. The year-to-date sharp outperformers have been consistent and
should continue to advance. These include commodities, including copper and oil, Emerging
Markets, Japan in the Developed Markets, Small-cap relative to Large-cap shares, Value versus

Growth, and more cyclically-oriented companies versus more defensive. This current consistent
outperformance informs us that we are still early- to mid-cycle in a supply-led expansion. We are
still early in the build out of the supply that is needed for the natural demand that is there. New
infrastructure is needed to handle the new economy that is progressing and quickly innovating.
This infrastructure needs commodities, all forms of energy, battery storage, minerals, metals,

recycled water, gases, chemicals, and all the compute and cloud capacity, including memory
and other components to handle the path toward mass tokenization. Moreover, the buildup of
government debt in the advanced economies as fiscal deficits widen is forcing large institutional
investors (including central banks and other government entities) to diversify their reserve
bases. This has increased the interest in gold holdings. Countries that have a vast amount of

natural resources, especially oil and gas, are in high demand by investors, which is obvious when
examining year-to-date outperformers. The bottom line is that the markets are telling us that we
are still early- to mid-cycle in this massive buildout, which will require a wide collection of hard
assets, Infrastructure (old and new), land, and within Equities, a more diversified approach to
exposure—particularly areas that are more cyclical- and Value-oriented. We expect this view to

extend itself well into the foreseeable future if not years.
In Fixed Income, it may seem more complex, but it is actually easier to understand what the
markets are telling us, in our opinion. The backup in
yields is “real yield” driven, which is more due
to higher nominal growth than to concerns of long-run inflation. This is evident in the Treasury
inflation protection market, break-evens in general and the ratio of Treasury yields relative to
other Go

vernment bond markets in the developed world. However, rising oil and gas prices and
inflation gauges that are still above the preferred level are usually the excuses for yields backing
up. Deficit concerns are a more plausible reason f
or yields rising but the main driver, in our view,
is still abo
ve average nominal growth. The yield curve and rates in general are still adjusting to
this new economic landscape that is very different than those of the most recent past.

Index Definitions
Securities indexes assume reinvestment of all distributions and interest payments. Indexes are unmanaged and do not take into account fees or expenses. It is not possible to
invest directly in an index. Indexes are all based in U.S. dollars.
S&P 500 Index is a stock market index tracking the stock performance of 500 leading companies listed on stock exchanges in the United States.

Bloomberg Commodity Index designed to provide liquid and diversified exposure to commodities via futures contracts. Is calculated on an excess return basis and reflects commodity
futures price movements.
MSCI Emerging Markets Index captures large and mid cap representation across 24 Emerging Markets countries. With 1,205 constituents, the index covers approximately 85% of the free
float adjusted market capitalization in each country.
MSCI Japan Index is designed to measure the performance of the large and mid cap segments of the Japanese market.

MSCI Pacific ex Japan Index captures large and mid cap representation across 4 of 5 Developed Markets (DM) countries in the Pacific region (excluding Japan). With 93 constituents, the index
covers approximately 85% of the free float-adjusted market capitalization in each country.
MSCI All-Country World Index is a premier benchmark representing global equity performance, covering large and mid-cap stocks across 23 developed and 24 emerging markets.

MSCI World Index ex U.S. captures large and mid cap representation across 22 of 23 Developed Markets countries--excluding the United States..
MSCI USA Momentum Index is based on MSCI USA Index, its parent index, which captures large and mid-cap stocks of the U.S. market. It is designed to reflect the performance of an equity
momentum strategy by emphasizing stocks with high price momentum, while maintaining reasonably high trading liquidity, investment capacity and moderate index turnover.

U.S. Large-cap Growth/Russell 1000 Growth Index measures the performance of the large-cap growth segment of the U.S. equity universe. It includes those Russell 1000 companies with
relatively higher price-to-book ratios, higher I/B/E/S forecast medium term (2 year) growth and higher sales per share historical growth (5 years).
U.S. Small-cap/Russell 2000 Index measures the performance of the small-cap segment of the U.S. Equity universe.
MSCI USA High Dividend Yield Index is designed to reflect the performance of equities in the MSCI USA Index (excluding REIT s) with higher dividend income and quality characteristics than

average dividend yields that are both sustainable and persistent.
U.S. Mid Cap/Russell Midcap Index measures the performance of the mid-cap segment of the U.S. Equity universe. The Russell Midcap Index is a subset of the Russell 1000® Index. It
includes approximately 800 of the smallest securities based on a combination of their market cap and current index membership.
MSCI USA Value Index captures large and mid-cap U.S. securities exhibiting overall value style characteristics.

MSCI USA Equal Weighted Index represents an alternative weighting scheme to its market cap weighted parent index, the MSCI USA Index. At each quarterly rebalance date, all index
constituents are weighted equally, effectively removing the influence of each constituent’s current price.
MSCI USA Minimum Volatility Index aims to reflect the performance characteristics of a minimum variance strategy applied to the large and mid-cap USA equity universe.

of all investors.
Asset allocation, diversification and rebalancing do not ensure a profit or protect against loss in declining markets.
Investments have varying degrees of risk. Some of the risks involved with equity securities include the possibility that the value of the stocks may fluctuate in response to events specific to

the companies or markets, as well as economic, political or social events in the U.S. or abroad. Small cap and mid cap companies pose special risks, including possible illiquidity and greater price
volatility than funds consisting of larger, more established companies. Investing in fixed-income securities may involve certain risks, including the credit quality of individual issuers, possible
prepayments, market or economic developments and yields and share price fluctuations due to changes in interest rates. When interest rates go up, bond prices typically drop, and vice versa.

Investments in high-yield bonds (sometimes referred to as “junk bonds”) offer the potential for high current income and attractive total return, but involves certain risks. Changes in economic
conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect a junk bond issuer’s ability to make principal and interest payments. Income from investing in municipal bonds is generally exempt
from Federal and state taxes for residents of the issuing state. While the interest income is tax-exempt, any capital gains distributed are taxable to the investor. Income for some investors

may be subject to the Federal Alternative Minimum Tax (AMT). Treasury bills are less volatile than longer-term fixed income securities and are guaranteed as to timely payment of principal and
interest by the U.S. government. Bonds are subject to interest rate, inflation and credit risks. Investments in foreign securities (including ADRs) involve special risks, including foreign currency
risk and the possibility of substantial volatility due to adverse political, economic or other developments. These risks are magnified for investments made in emerging markets. Investments in

a certain industry or sector may pose additional risk due to lack of diversification and sector concentration. There are special risks associated with an investment in commodities, such as gold,
including market price fluctuations, regulatory changes, interest rate changes, credit risk, economic changes and the impact of adverse political or financial factors.
Alternative investments are speculative and involve a high degree of risk.
Alternative investments are intended for qualified investors only. Alternative Investments such as derivatives, hedge funds, private credit, private equity funds, and funds of funds can result

in higher return potential but also higher loss potential. Changes in economic conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect your investments. Before you invest in alternative
investments, you should consider your overall financial situation, how much money you have to invest, your need for liquidity, and your tolerance for risk.

Subject: ECONOMIC WEEK AHEAD: October 19 - 23
Date:    1 hour ago
ECONOMIC WEEK AHEAD: October 19 - 23

YARDENI QUICKTAKES
ECONOMIC WEEK AHEAD:
October 19 - 23
By Ed Yardeni & Toby Hearst • 18 Oct 2026
View in browser

### Page 2

Photo by Monica Sauro / Unsplash

### Page 3

Last week’s inflation reports came in hot. September’s CPI rose 0.6%
m/m, lifting headline inflation from 3.4% y/y in August to 3.7%. Core CPI
rose 0.3% m/m, taking its y/y rate from 2.4% to 2.6%. Both topped
expectations. PPI final demand jumped 1.3% m/m. That pushed its y/y
rate to 6.3%, the highest since 2022. Retail sales rose 0.8% m/m, though
just 0.2% excluding gas stations.
Odds of a 25bps hike at the October 27-28 FOMC meeting fell from 22%

before the CPI report to 17%, despite the hot inflation data. Fed Chair
Kevin Warsh offered no signal on October's meeting at the IMF and World
Bank annual meetings in Bangkok on Friday. “I don’t prejudge meetings,”
he said. Odds of a December hike rose. We still expect the Fed to reverse
the remaining two of last year’s three 25bps “insurance” cuts. Fed officials
are now in their pre-meeting blackout period.
This week’s data calendar is light, with earnings season taking center

stage. Friday’s flash PMIs are the main US releases. The Fed’s Financial
Stability Report and Canada’s federal budget are tentatively due Tuesday.
Here’s more:
(1) ADP & Unemployment Claims. ADP’s weekly employment reading
(Tue) follows a rise in the four-week average to 24,750 for the week ended
September 26, the highest since early June and up from a late-July
bottom of 8,250 (chart).
Initial jobless claims (Thu) follow last week’s 199,000, with the four-week

average at 198,500 (chart). Continuing claims fell to 1,710,000 for the

### Page 4

week ended October 2, matching their four-week average. The labor
market continues to improve.
(2) Flash PMIs. S&P Global’s flash M-PMI and NM-PMI for October (Fri)
follow September readings of 55.9 and 58.8 (chart). The services prices
component is worth watching after ISM’s NM-PMI prices-paid index
jumped to 70.7 in September, the highest since July 2022.
Flash PMIs for Australia and Japan (Thu) and for the Eurozone, Germany,
France, and the UK (Fri) also land this week. September’s M-PMIs were

52.9 for the Eurozone and 52.1 for the UK.
(3) Earnings. The earnings reporting season picks up this week. Netflix,
GE Aerospace, and Northrop Grumman report on Tuesday. Tesla and
Meta, the first of the Magnificent-7 to report, follow on Wednesday along

### Page 5

with Lam Research, IBM, and Texas Instruments. Spotify, Bloom Energy,
Western Digital, Lockheed Martin, and Blackstone report on Thursday.
ASML and TSMC posted record Q3 results last week. ASML’s net sales
rose to €11.7 billion from €9.3 billion in Q2, with the gross margin up to
57.5% from 54.0%. TSMC’s revenue jumped 50.9% y/y. Analysts’
consensus estimates imply Q3-2026 operating EPS growth for S&P 500
companies of 24.0% y/y (chart).
(4) Global. China’s Q3 GDP (Mon) follows Q2’s 4.3% y/y growth (chart).

We expect a moderate pickup in activity. China’s unemployment rate for
September is also due, following August’s 5.2% rate.
UK CPI (Wed) for September follows August’s 3.1% y/y headline and
2.6% core (chart). Rising energy and input costs point to a further pickup,

### Page 6

with the input PPI up 8.2% y/y in Q2. Bank of England Governor Andrew
Bailey speaks later that day. UK labor market data (Tue) and PPI (Wed)
are also due.
Canada’s CPI (Mon) for September follows August’s 3.0% y/y headline
and 2.3% core (chart).

## Document 298: First Impressions_ NZ household incomes, assets and savings update - June quarter 2026 (2026-10-24)

Source PDF: all report pdf/First Impressions_ NZ household incomes, assets and savings update - June quarter 2026 (2026-10-24).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-10-24
Pages: 10
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

#consumer #newzealand
First Impressions: NZ household incomes, assets and savings
update - June quarter 2026
By Satish Ranchhod
Senior Economist, Westpac NZ
10:24 October 08 2026 Share
New Zealand incomes and savings are rising, as is the value of households’ financial
assets. However, the value of housing assets remains flat.
Key takeouts from Stats NZ’s latest update on household incomes, assets and savings (June quarter
2026)

### Page 2

Household incomes
Disposable income levels in the economy grew by 5.2% in the year to June, down from 6.5% in the
year to March

Savings and wealth
Household savings have continued to rise, climbing by $2.5b over the past quarter.

Overall household wealth levels were up 1.9% over the past year. Under the surface, the make-up of
household wealth continues to shift.

Interest costs 
Overall household income levels are continuing to rise. Much of that is due to increases in
entrepreneurial earnings (which is in part due to increased earnings in the agricultural sector). Wage
and salary growth has been more limited but has picked up.
Household savings levels have been rising for three years now, with another solid rise over the past
three months. 
Overall household wealth levels have risen modestly. Although the value of households’ financial
assets is continuing to rise, housing assets have essentially shown no growth since late 2023.
Household spending on interest costs rose just slightly in the June quarter. That’s the first increase in
two years. The easing in average borrowing costs seen over the past few years has come to a close
with the RBNZ’s hiking cycle now in train.
Underpinning the rise in overall income levels have been continued increases in entrepreneurial
earnings, which are up 12% over the past year. That will include stronger earnings in the agricultural
sector, which has been boosted by the strength in commodity export prices. That lift in agricultural
earnings has been reflected in stronger economic confidence and spending in many rural regions.
We expect commodity export earnings will remain firm over the year ahead. 
In contrast, wage and salary earnings are up just 2.7% over the past year. That’s up from 1.8% in the
year to March, but it’s a fairly muted rise, especially given the continued increases in the population
and inflation of 4.1%.
June’s large increase in savings builds on the solid increases seen over the past three years.
The savings rate in the June quarter was 3.7% of disposable incomes, down just slightly from last
quarter.
The value of households’ financial assets has continued to rise and is up 4.6% over the past year. 
However, the value of housing and land assets is down 0.9% over the past year, and it has remained
essentially unchanged since late 2023. We expect housing market conditions will remain soft over
the coming year, limiting increases in overall household wealth. 
Households’ spending on interest costs rose just slightly in the June quarter, climbing by 0.3%.
While that’s a relatively small change, it is the first increase that we’ve seen in two years.

### Page 3

Related articles

Media Contact:
Satish Ranchhod, Senior Economist
P: +64 9 336 5668
M: +64 21 710 852
Over the past few years, many borrowers saw their debt servicing costs fall as they rolled off earlier
high mortgage rates and onto lower ones. That helped to support household spending.
However, the easing in average borrowing cost has now ended. Recent months have seen mortgage
rates pushing higher as the RBNZ has begun to hike the Official Cash Rate, with further hikes
expected over the coming year. 
Looking ahead, we don’t expect a significant increase in average household borrowing costs.
However, we won’t see the same support for spending that we did over the past few years.

Jeff Pu, CFA

Market to remain choppy: Tech equities were volatile in the past month, with rotation 
toward hyperscaler and internet names. That said , s emi and memory (Korea) 
weakness reflected lower risk appetite due to rising bond yields, AI monetization/ARR 
visibility and L-T AI debates (i.e., Broadcom CEO warned that capital may now be a 
bigger constraint), even as company-specific catalysts continued to be strong. On the 
other hand, the positive was the acceleration of AI model scaling (i.e., Meta’s Muse 
Wonder, Gemini 4, etc). That said, w e view the pullback primarily as a reset in 
expectations rather than the evidence of AI deterioration. Despite light positioning, we 
expect the markets will remain choppy in the near term but would  accumulate low 
valuation & sustainable names  (i.e., HBM, non-TSMC foundries, CPUs, CPOs, etc) . 
In our preferred list, we added CLS, while removing MTK and Hon Hai.  
U.S. roadshow takeaways (week of Oct 5): Investor tone skewed neutral to slightly 
cautious, driven by conservative top ‑down macro views, U.S. election, and ongoing 
debates around the L-T AI demand. Positioning in AI infrastructure has come off peak 
levels. Even  so, many investors would accumulate on pullbacks, with interest 
concentrated in “Made in U.S.” names (INTC, LITE, COHR), U.S. memory suppliers, 
TSMC, and selective bottleneck themes — including ASE, ABF substrates, 
MRVL/SMTC, and Japanese MLCCs. 
OCP & earnings season in focus: The week is anchored by AI/semis — SEMICON 
West, OCP, and earnings from ASML, TSMC, etc. OCP reinforced the industry’s pivot 
toward rack‑scale infrastructure, with inference and agentic workloads driving demand 
across compute, memory, and networking. Scale‑up optics, NPO/CPO, and 800VDC 
power were key themes, while AMD’s CPU outlook and Helios/Helion underscored a 
secular CPU uptrend. Nvidia ’s use of PTFE for NVLink boards further supports our 
positive stance on PTFE (see PCB Spec in 2 H27, Oct 5).  On earnings, ASML 
delivered largely in -line results, GM and OP above consensus with revenue slightly 
miss, and raised full year guidance again and is exploring ways to increase capacity 
by >30% 2028 to fulfill strong customer demand, also extending the order visibility 
beyond 2028. For TSMC, we anticipate an inline quarter. 
September monthly sales/data: TSMC sales -0.6% MoM, slightly ahead. Rubin to 
accelerate and T3 ramp. PC forecast trimmed. Substrate/CCL upcycle with 
pricing/margin expansion. Aspeed revenue +101% YoY. Details on page 5.

GFHK Tech Important Stock Calls  
In the past one month, our key reports and stock calls are: 
Jeff Pu on ASIC Updates on TPU v9, reported date on Oct 13: We expect near-term MTK headwinds 
from TPU v9 delays but remain constructive on MTK/MRVL longer term. Humufish’s redesign could 
push tape-out to Jan -27, MP tape -out to 2Q27 and shipments to 1Q28, while EMIB -T yields could 
recover from 45–50%. Rising TPU allocations to Anthropic could constrain Gemini compute, though 
higher internal allocation in 2H26 may support future models. SemiAnalysis expects Anthropic/OpenAI 
to each add 5 –10GW in 2027 vs. a few GW for Google. For TPU v10 (4Q28 /1Q29), MTK/GUC’s 
Icefish remains the only confirmed mainstream SKU, while Broadcom-led Merope faces AMD/Marvell 
competition, with both potentially testing EMIB-T. 
Michelle Jing on PCB Spec in 2H27, reported date on Oct 5: Some market watchers expect Nvidia 
to shift from PTFE to hydrocarbon resin (HC) for the VR300 NVL576 switch tray. HC—one of the glass 
free alternatives within PTFE hybrid architectures—has only recently passed SI testing and has yet to 
enter PCB MP, leaving it roughly three months behind PTFE in qualification. On the contrary, we 
believe that PTFE offers superior performance for high frequency, high speed designs. With VR300 
NVL576 shipments expected in 2H27 and the final configuration likely set by end 26 —and with a 
leading Chinese PCB ma ker already achieving meaningful PTFE hybrid yields —we maintain that 
PTFE hybrid remains the most probable solution for next year’s switch board. 
Alicia Xia on CPO Acceleration , reported date on  Sept 29 : Nvidia’s CPO scale -out switch is 
progressing faster than expected, with FAU yields improving. We forecast CPO switch shipments at 
15k/16k/30k in 4Q26/1Q27/2Q27 and 100k in 2027. For Nvidia/AWS scale -up, CPO and NPO 
continue in parallel, with full NPO adoption expected at NVL576 launch. Feynman is also devel oping 
both variants, with CPO potentially using QM6 + six 6.4T OEs. We shift AWS Trainium 4 launch to 
1Q28 and forecast global scale-up OE demand at 6.5m/42m in 2027E/28E. 
Jeff Pu on NAND Re -acceleration, reported date on Sept 20 : According to TrendForce, NAND 
demand is stronger than expected as U.S. CSPs scale AI infrastructure, with eSSD demand nearly 
2x prior forecasts. We raise NAND contract price growth to +21%/+17%/+15% QoQ in 
3Q26/4Q26/1Q27, while hyperscaler inventories decline. Supply remains disci plined at 15%/21%/20% 
YoY bit growth in 2025 –27. Demand is supported by agentic AI, vector databases and KV -cache 
offloading, where eSSD offers lower cost/bit than DRAM and better power efficiency than HDD. 
Jeff Pu on TSMC 3Q26 Preview , reported date on  Oct 7 : TSMC shares hit record highs on 
expectations ahead of the print and potential Terafab collaboration. We expect largely inline 3Q26 
results, capex and full-year guidance, with softer 4Q26 margin (67.2%). Management should reaffirm 
2026 capex at the ceiling and mid-40s YoY growth, while we forecast 37% growth in 2027 on sustained 
AI/CPU/networking demand and price hikes. Key focuses include margins, long -term capex/U.S. 
expansion, large-reticle packaging and A14. We raise 2026E/27E EPS by 2%/8% on hig her N2/N3 
capacity and operating leverage, and roll to 20x 2027E P/E, lifting TP to NT$2,960.

September Monthly Data  
TSMC September sales of NT$511.9bn, -0.6% MoM and +54.6% YoY, with total 3Q revenue reaching 
NT$1.49T, +17.6 QoQ, slightly ahead of expectations of +14%. The solid performance was well 
supported by ASIC, A20 Pro, and Rubin. For 3Q26, we expect gross margin to be 67.8%, higher than 
guidance of 65-67% and consensus of 66.4% . For 4Q26, we expect another 12% QoQ growth with 
GM at 67.2%, both ahead of street’s estimates of +10% QoQ and 66% margin. Ahead of the earnings 
call, key focus include gross margin trajectory, long -term capex discipline, and the execution of its 
U.S. fab expansion. 
Rubin to accelerate:  Hon Hai revenue last month was NT$1.16T, +26 % MoM, +38% YoY, mainly 
driven by iPhone build.  Quanta and Wistron delivered -23% and -17% MoM respectively, due to AI 
racks’ shipping timing differences. Of note, Pegatron surprised the market with +143.2% MoM revenue 
surge fueled by initial GB300 rack shipments to neocloud customers including Lambda and Together 
AI. This reaffirms our thesis of robust NVL72 order momentum across Neoclouds. Looking ahead, 
VR200 is ramping now, with volume deployment accelerating into 2027. We maintain our forecast of 
75k and 92k NVL72 (GB+VR) rack shipments for 2026 and 2027, respectively. Concurrently, Wiwynn 
delivered another solid month with revenue expanding 20.0% MoM, underpinne d by resilient GP 
server demand alongside the ramp of Trainium 3. 
ASIC ramping up: On design service vendors fronts, driven  by T3 ramp, Alchip’s Sep revenue 
reached a record NT$9.9 8bn. Backed by secured incremental 3nm wafer allocation and improving 
yields, management raised its 2027 revenue guidance, now targeting growth above the previous 50% 
YoY baseline. That said, we push out T4 shipment timeline into 1Q28. GUC delivered a strong beat 
with monthly revenue reaching a record NT$7.145bn (+22.4% MoM, +97.8 % YoY), driven by robust 
Axion 2 CPU demand. Beyond its engagement with top 4 CSPs, multi -year order visibility into 2027 
and beyond is further bolstered by high-margin (likely TK1) automotive ASIC designs. 
PC forecast trimmed: NB shipments from top 5 ODMs were 9.7m in Sep, with 3Q26 shipments down 
17% QoQ and 26% YoY. Global PC OEM shipments contracted 20.1% YoY in 3Q26, worse than 
expected, post robust 1H26 pull -ins with high pricing points and weak macro. For 2027, we project 
the industry will decline a further 5% YoY. On the competitive front, tier -1 vendors such as Lenovo, 
Dell, and HP are not immune to this downturn, while entry-level laptops (low cost models from China) 
continue to see stronger relative demand.  
Substrate/CCL Upcycle Intact: Unimicron continued to deliver another all-time high sales with +5% 
MoM and 65% YoY. Supported by ABF upward pricing of ~15% in 2H26 (likely more next year) and 
higher mix of AI products, we expect a meaningful GM uptick for this quarter with momentum to sustain 
into 2027 . In the meantime, ZengDing’s revenue rose 28% MoM and 36% YoY mainly driven by  
iPhone, 1.6T mSAP, and Rubin’s HDI. On the CCL front, bolstered by ongoing price hikes and strong 
demand from GP servers, ITEC revenue grew 15.5% MoM and 111% YoY, and NanYa Plastics 
recorded +12.5% MoM and +55% YoY. Meanwhile, EMC and TUC both posted another single digit 
growth MoM on sales.

### Page 11

Sector Report | Technology

Downstream tech – Smartphone & Apple  
We maintain Global smartphone forecast of -14% in 2026  
According to IDC, the preliminary 2Q26 smartphone data points to a still ‑soft market with uneven 
regional dynamics, and their commentary continues to emphasize structural rather than cyclical 
headwinds. Smartphone shipments remain under pressure from weak replacement cycles, cautious 
consumer spending, and limited breakthrough features. IDC typically highlights that “growth is 
concentrated in the premium tier,” driven by ecosystem lock ‑in, while the mass market remains 
challenged by price sensitivity. They also tend to note that China and parts of emerging markets show 
only modest stabilization, not a full recovery, and that vendors are prioritizing margin discipline over 
volume.  
Overall, by factoring in higher iPhone 17/17 Pro volume, we fine tune our global smartphone shipment 
forecast to -14% YoY from -15% YoY. 
For 2027, we expect global smartphone shipments to be flattish YoY. That said, 2026 -2027 will be 
one of the most challenging periods for the industry, driven primarily by sharply higher memory prices, 
which have already pushed OEMs to reduce shipments and raise device prices.

### Page 12

Sector Report | Technology

iPhone 18 Pro/18 PM demand lukewarm  
iPhone 18 Pro/Pro Max wait-time lukewarm: iPhone 18 Pro and iPhone 18 Pro Max pre-orders began 
on Sept 12 in the U.S. and many other countries. Generally speaking, the wait-time appears lukewarm, 
with: 1) 3–4 weeks for 256GB/512GB Pro Max across all colors, as well as Burgundy 1TB in the U.S.；
2) 2–3 weeks for Glacier 256GB/512GB Pro Max in China; 3) 1–2 weeks for Black/Glacier 1TB Pro 
Max in the U.S. ；4) immediate availability for Silver 1TB and mos t 2TB Pro Max models in the U.S, 
as well as most iPhone 18 Pro configurations except for Burgundy 256GB in the U.S. We attribute the 
lukewarm wait-time to limited spec upgrade, higher pricing (especially for large storage models), etc. 
We also expect the Pro Max to be impacted by iPhone Duo and the criticism of heavy weight.

Downstream tech – PC 
Global PC units down to -12% in 2026, followed by another decline in 2027 
On Oct 8, IDC reported 3Q26 global PC shipments fell 20.1% YoY, missing street expectations of a 
10–15% decline. The sharper contraction was primarily driven by front -loaded demand in 1H26, 
elevated ASPs, and broader macro weakness. This aligns with our prior view that ODM build 
momentum moderated starting in end -2Q26, amid the rising concerns over channel inventory. 
Meanwhile, the top 5 ODMs also saw 3Q26 shipments decline 26% YoY. Looking ahead, we model 
4Q26 shipments to continue 20% YoY drop (flattish QoQ), underperforming normal seasonality. 
On the component front, we expect memory de -spec to normalize. End -users still require sufficient 
DRAM capacity (16GB+) to handle on -device AI workloads, and PC DDR 5 supply constraints have 
eased compared to Server’s. On pricing, new models will still have higher pricing points to pass 
through 2H component cost increases across memory, CPU, etc. That said, we anticipate price hikes 
will remain measured vs 1H26 given sluggish end-market demand. 
Overall, reflecting weaker than expected 3Q26 results post pull -ins and a muted 4Q outlook, we trim 
our 2026 PC shipment forecast to -12% YoY. For 2027, we project shipments to decline further by 5% 
YoY, as higher ASP and persistent macro headwinds continue to weigh on demand.
